# Progress

Current milestone: M7

How to use this file: `/plan-milestone M<n>` writes a milestone's tasks here, `/run-milestone` (or `/next-task`, one at a time) works through them, and `docs-keeper` ticks each task with evidence. Scripts parse this format, so keep it: a `Current milestone:` line, `## M<n> — Title` headings, and tasks written as `- [ ] M<n>.<k> Title` with indented `AC:`, `Tests:` and `Evidence:` lines, plus optional `Checkpoint:` and `Blocked:` lines (ADR-065). Open technical debt, known limits and unverified items are listed in `docs/TECH_DEBT.md`.

## M0 — Foundation and tooling

Status: accepted (2026-10-04)

Branch: `m0-foundation`. Decisions for this plan: ADR-022 to ADR-043 (dependencies in ADR-031, CI operating systems in ADR-032, Windows Ctrl+Break verification in ADR-040, data folder ownership check in ADR-043).

- [x] M0.1 Project scaffold, dependencies, pytest markers
  - AC: AC-M0.7 (part); `uv sync` works on a fresh clone; ruff, mypy (strict, `app`), pytest with `--strict-markers` and the markers from spec 7.2; empty package tree from spec 2.13; `jobpipeline --version`; approved dependency set recorded in an ADR.
  - Tests: unit `test_package_exposes_version_from_metadata`, `test_markers_registered_and_strict` (pytester), `test_live_tests_skipped_without_env`, `test_live_tests_run_with_env`, `test_cli_version_prints_package_version_and_exits_0`.
  - Evidence: 2026-10-03, `verify.sh quick` PASS (format, lint, mypy strict on 19 files, 28 fast tests; smoke has no tests yet). Fresh clone: `uv sync --locked` OK, `jobpipeline --version` prints `jobpipeline 0.1.0`, 28 passed. Added after review: `test_unmarked_tests_named_live_are_not_skipped` (live gating now uses the marker, not keywords) and `test_cli_console_script_points_to_main`; pytest uses `--import-mode=importlib`. Ruff excludes `.claude/` and `docs/` (existing hook scripts and spec code samples are not project code); formatting the hooks is left to the user.

If forbidding not-yet-installed vendor packages errors, those contracts are added in the milestone that installs the package (ADR), never weakened.
  - Tests: integration `test_ac_m0_6_lint_imports_passes_on_repo`; `test_ac_m0_6_violation_detected[<contract>]` with one violating fixture per contract (stored as `.py.txt`, run in a temp copy).

- [x] M0.3 Spike: pre-commit with ruff and gitleaks
  - AC: AC-M0.7 (part); `.pre-commit-config.yaml` with pinned ruff-format, ruff check and gitleaks; tests build key-shaped strings at runtime, no gitleaks allowlist.
  - Tests: unit `test_precommit_config_has_pinned_ruff_and_gitleaks`; manual check that a staged file with a fake key is blocked (recorded as evidence).
  - Evidence: 2026-10-03, `verify.sh quick` PASS (86 fast tests); `pre-commit run --all-files` and `pre-commit run --hook-stage manual gitleaks-history` pass on the repo. Tests: `test_precommit_config_has_pinned_ruff_and_gitleaks`, `test_ruff_hook_version_matches_locked_ruff`, `test_ruff_honours_excludes_when_given_file_names`, `test_gitleaks_runs_without_allowlist`, `test_no_inline_gitleaks_allow_comments`, `test_gitleaks_history_hook_scans_commits`. Manual check in a throwaway clone: committing a staged file with a randomly generated fake Anthropic-shaped key was blocked (gitleaks RuleID `anthropic-api-key`, commit exit 1, no commit created); a fake key already committed was caught by `gitleaks-history` (exit 1). Review finding: the per-commit gitleaks hook scans only staged changes, so CI and `verify.sh full` use the `gitleaks-history` hook (manual stage). Install now includes `uv run pre-commit install` (CLAUDE.md, spec 4.5).

Security review found a real gap: claude_agent_sdk's own loggers format exception text (possibly holding the key) into record.msg, and only handlers the app installs cleaned it (pytest caplog, logging.lastResort, any later handler saw the key). User decision 2026-10-06: reduce SDK records at creation. Fix: app/logging_config.py install_sdk_record_reduction() wraps the log record factory so every claude_agent_sdk.* record is reduced to level, logger name and exception type when created (marker SDK_REDUCED_ATTR holds the type name; the message is always rebuilt so a pre-marked record can't keep text); configure_logging installs it, LoggingSetup.close restores it; the CLI installs it before logging setup for the process lifetime (no shutdown window); tests/conftest.py installs it session-wide (covers caplog and the M1.25 live tests). Handler filters kept as defence in depth. Proven: with the install a no-op, the stderr test fails with 80 leaks. Code, security (two rounds, final risk LOW) reviews done; fixes applied (status assertions on every POST in the failing-keychain block, exact failure messages per script, restart credentials_sent, header case in self-test, tools moved out, uvicorn logger state restored). Left for the user: values passed with `extra=` on SDK loggers are added after the record factory runs, so only the app's handler filters strip them (the pinned SDK passes none; re-check on SDK upgrades); the real-subprocess fake-CLI stderr variant is not covered (it would need a Windows-only skip, which needs your approval; the stderr path is covered through the replaying transport); the M1.25 live tests should also scan the per-call CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR and working folder before they're removed; tests/unit/modules/ai/test_claude_provider.py adds SdkRecordFilter to pytest's shared caplog handler without removing it at teardown. ## M2 — Task runner, live updates and usage budgets

Status: accepted (2026-10-11)

Branch: `m2-task-runner`. Decisions for this plan: ADR-076 (stream authorisation, practice task, budget services and unit, provider-reported usage, cycles, data-folder lock, retry, scope moves). Security review on M2.1, M2.2, M2.4, M2.13, M2.16, M2.17, M2.18, M2.26; UX review on M2.20, M2.22–M2.25.

- [x] M2.1 Spike: event stream authorisation, streaming and clean shutdown
  - AC: groundwork for AC-M2.9; ADR-076 item 1: `GET /events` needs the token (not a page), has no side effects, returns `text/event-stream` with `Cache-Control: no-store`, starts with a `resync` event and sends a comment keepalive about every 15 s; the stream ends when shutdown starts (the CLI wraps uvicorn's `Server.handle_exit` to set an app shutdown event before uvicorn waits for connections; `timeout_graceful_shutdown` about 5 s as backstop); `SecurityMiddleware` and `SetupRedirectMiddleware` pass chunks through unbuffered; a minimal `fetch`-based reader in app.js, loaded on app pages only (not the guide), reads the token from body `hx-headers` and sends `X-JP-Token`; a reusable ASGI stream driver for tests (httpx's ASGITransport buffers, TestClient blocks on endless streams); a new ADR amending ADR-029 and ADR-039, spec 2.9.12 and 2.12.

Independent of the runner tasks.

  - Evidence: 2026-10-10 `verify.sh quick` PASS; ruff, mypy, lint-imports clean; tests/unit/modules/ai, tests/unit/tasks, test_runner_app.py and tests/integration/failures 593 passed. tests/unit/modules/ai/test_claude_provider_timeouts.py: `test_cancel_while_cli_silent_closes_client_promptly`, `test_run_overall_timeout_maps_to_network_unavailable_and_closes`, `test_run_timeout_from_wiring[3 Claude kinds]`, keyword-only `run_timeout`, and the earlier of run and connection deadlines wins (mutation-checked); tests/integration/failures/test_claude_cancel_subprocess.py `test_cancel_while_fake_cli_silent_leaves_no_subprocess[silent|silent_ignore_eof]` and `test_run_timeout_kills_fake_cli` (the fake CLI's pid is gone after cancel or timeout; new fake scenarios `silent`, `silent_ignore_eof` and an opt-in pid file, tests only). `ClaudeSdkProvider(run_timeout=PROVIDER_RUN_TIMEOUT)` from wiring; on expiry NetworkUnavailable (detail "timeout"), client closed once in the calling task, call folder removed. `interrupt()` not used (it waits for a reply a silent CLI never sends). Floor raised: task-type timeouts must exceed `TASK_TIMEOUT_FLOOR` = PROVIDER_RUN_TIMEOUT + 25 s close + 5 s (app/wiring.py), because a call can take up to 25 s to close after its deadline. Code review (deadline combination untested; floor ignored the close time) and security review (LOW; subprocess always reaped, folder removed, type-only errors, fake CLI never on the production path) — fixes applied. Note: the cancel-while-silent tests also pass on the old code (regression guards). The SDK waits 5 s after closing stdin before terminating a CLI that ignores it, so such a cancel takes about 5 s; shortening it would need private SDK APIs (not done; recheck on SDK upgrades).

Semantics: `evaluate` takes the count used so far and checks count + 1; with block_at = 100, exactly 100 requests run and the 101st is blocked; precedence Blocked, CapBlocked, Warn (highest unshown threshold), Note, Allow; caps may be set up to the allowance, but one above block_at never triggers. Code review approved; fixes applied. Notes for M2.15: compare cycle bounds in UTC or by start_date, never by wall time; CapBlocked.offer_fast means "offer Fast" after evaluating again on Fast. - [x] M2.21 Failure injection with a real process kill
  - AC: AC-M2.2, AC-M2.6; tests only, in `tests/integration/failures/test_process_kill.py` and `test_provider_failures.py`, using the M2.3 kill and restart helper.
  - Tests: integration/failures `test_ac_m2_2_kill_during_task_leaves_interrupted_after_restart`, `test_ac_m2_2_no_automatic_retry_after_restart` (attempt stays 1 and no provider call happens during a wait), `test_ac_m2_2_retry_after_restart_succeeds`, `test_ac_m2_6_kill_during_provider_call_keeps_reserved_usage_counted`, `test_network_failure_ends_failed_with_mapped_reason_and_counted`, `test_invalid_output_twice_ends_failed_counting_two_requests`; e2e `test_interrupted_task_shows_retry_after_restart`.
  - Evidence: 2026-10-10 `verify.sh quick` PASS (3123 fast, 10 smoke; ruff, mypy, lint-imports clean); VERIFY quick: PASS. Tests only; nothing under `app/` changed. New files: `tests/integration/failures/test_process_kill.py` (4 tests), `tests/integration/failures/test_provider_failures.py` (2 tests), `tests/e2e/test_task_restart.py` (1 test). Names as in the task: `test_ac_m2_2_kill_during_task_leaves_interrupted_after_restart`, `test_ac_m2_2_no_automatic_retry_after_restart`, `test_ac_m2_2_retry_after_restart_succeeds`, `test_ac_m2_6_kill_during_provider_call_keeps_reserved_usage_counted`, `test_network_failure_ends_failed_with_mapped_reason_and_counted`, `test_invalid_output_twice_ends_failed_counting_two_requests`, `test_interrupted_task_shows_retry_after_restart`. The kill tests start the real app (`start_app`), start a slow practice task through `POST /tasks/practice`, and wait with `wait_until` until the task is running with exactly one unfinished usage record. Then they SIGKILL it with `RunningApp.kill()` and restart on the same folders (`restart=True`). After the kill and after the restart the usage rows are identical column for column, and `UsageService` counts them as (requests 2, unfinished 1, breakdown 2). The provider-failure tests run in-process (practice outcomes `network` and `invalid_output`). The task ends failed with kind NetworkUnavailable or InvalidModelOutput, and its mapped copy shows on the fragment and the panel (Retry only). Network leaves 1 usage row. Invalid output leaves 2 rows, both finished as failed (the scripted third answer is unused). The e2e test starts a slow task from Settings > Testing tools, kills the app, restarts it, and opens the panel by keyboard. It shows Interrupted, the reason and Retry, with no Cancel and no progress. Retry by keyboard requeues the task (db shows running, attempt 2). Cancel by keyboard ends it Cancelled. The item fits 390x844. Axe runs at phone and desktop (light) for the interrupted item. Each test was shown to fail under a temporary mutation of app code, and all mutations were reverted (`git diff --stat -- app` empty): recovery requeues instead of interrupting; the runner auto-retries interrupted tasks; a delayed auto-retry after another task finishes (only the sentinel test catches it); usage reserve dropped; unfinished usage no longer counted; recovery completes or deletes unfinished usage; retry not offered for interrupted; retry doesn't bump attempt; failed marked succeeded; failed usage record deleted; failed call left reserved; mapped copy replaced by the generic one; invalid-output retry not metered. Run times: `test_process_kill.py` about 25 s in total (retry-after-restart about 17 s, the AC-M2.6 test about 4.5 s); `test_provider_failures.py` 1.7 s; the e2e test about 3.4 s. Nothing is marked `slow`. The integration files passed 3 runs of 3, and the e2e test passed 5 of 5, also under concurrent load. Deviation: the criteria say "no provider call happens during a wait". The no-automatic-retry test uses no timed wait, because the rules forbid fixed sleeps. A sentinel quick practice task stands in for the wait. The runner claims oldest first, so a re-queued task would have run before the sentinel finished. The killed row and its usage rows are compared with what recovery left. The test cannot see a retry that fires later than that (for example from a timer). The immediate checks cover a re-queue at once, and `tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py` covers claim order in-process. The test's comment says so. `verify.sh full` was run twice and did not pass. Format, lint, mypy, module boundaries, critical coverage (runner 97.5% branches; security, detection, budgets and usage 100%), pre-commit and gitleaks-history passed, and the three new files pass inside it. The failures are in existing tests outside this task: (1) e2e `tests/e2e/test_tasks_panel.py::test_top_bar_stays_one_row_on_phones[390|360]` (M2.20) failed in both full runs. Run alone on a quiet machine it fails about 3 of 4 times with "Menu at y=-2185, Tasks at y=-2149", after the test sets the count text to "3 running, 12 queued". It passed 4 of 4 at M2.20. The new files are not loaded when it runs alone, so M2.21 does not cause it. (2) Once, under load while the code review ran tests at the same time, `tests/integration/failures/test_atomic_result.py::test_ac_m2_5_complete_saves_result_and_success_in_one_transaction` failed with `(seen,) = states` getting 0 values. It passes 5 of 5 on a quiet machine. Code review: approved, no critical findings. The one should-fix (the sentinel comment overstated what it proves) is fixed. Not changed (nice to have): the provider-failure tests compare the reason with the class constant rather than the literal copy (M2.19 pins the literals); the e2e test repeats `app.db` and `task_rows` instead of importing them; the e2e start button uses the pointer, the rest is keyboard only; axe for the interrupted item runs in light only (dark is covered by the M2.20 tests). Left for the user: decide how to fix the flaky M2.20 e2e test `test_top_bar_stays_one_row_on_phones` (an existing test, not edited by the agent). It must be fixed before M2 acceptance, which runs `verify.sh full`. Not yet diagnosed; possible causes, not checked, are a race with the event-stream refresh of the indicator text, or a real wrap of the long count at 360 and 390 px. Also harden the in-process race in `test_ac_m2_5_complete_saves_result_and_success_in_one_transaction`.

## M3 — Fact bank and CV import

Status: accepted (2026-10-19)

Branch: `m3-fact-bank`. Decisions for this plan: ADR-089 (ten larger tasks, dependencies, the `app/modules/extract` module, AC-M3.8 and Today, the refine honesty check, never-use facts and refine, mechanical near-duplicates, per-task tier list to M9, fake label). Security review on M3.1, M3.4, M3.5, M3.8; prompt review on M3.4, M3.8; UX review on M3.5, M3.6, M3.7, M3.9. Split points if a task stalls: M3.3 (search and filters), M3.4 (routes), M3.8 (the refine task and service).

- [x] M3.1 Extraction and uploads
  - AC: AC-M3.7 (oversized, wrong type, malformed); spec 2.9 rule 5; fictional CV fixtures in `tests/fixtures/cv/` made by script (single-column, two-column and table PDF, Word with tables, plain text, truncated, encrypted and image-only PDF, a zip that isn't Word, a Word file with an oversized entry) with no author metadata; new module `app/modules/extract/` (interface in `__init__.py`, vendor imports only in `providers/pdf.py` and `providers/docx.py`, two added import contracts); `sniff_type` decides by content; `extract_text(path, kind, timeout)` returns text or raises `UnreadableDocument`, `NoTextFound` or `ParseTimedOut`, parsing in a child process killed at the deadline; encrypted PDFs and oversized Word entries refused; `read_upload(request)` streams with a 10 MB cap that counts bytes and ignores `Content-Length`, takes one file part or a `text` field, and never uses the client filename as a path; adds `python-multipart`, `pypdf` and `python-docx` (ADR-089 item 2; come back to the user only if `pypdf` fails the fixtures).

Review regressions also cover: charset refusal gives 400 not 500, the child sees no environment, child CPU limit, 9 MB of text extracted, over-cap child answer, unpicklable extractor, exact-cap and cap+1 uploads, and a memory-only spy. Deps: pypdf 6.20.0 (`>=6.20`), python-docx 1.2.0 (`>=1.2,<2`), python-multipart 0.0.32; pypdf passed every fixture. New: `app/modules/extract/` (`sniff_type`, `extract_text`, `extractor_for`, `TextExtractor`; providers pdf, docx, text; `runner.py` runs parsing in a child process that is killed and reaped at the deadline, with a cleared environment, CPU limit timeout+5 s, stderr to devnull, a 16 MiB answer cap and status-byte answers, never unpickling child data); `app/api/uploads.py` (`read_upload`, `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES`, in memory only, non-UTF-8 charsets refused); seven error classes in `app/domain/errors.py` with 413 and 400 plain-text backstop handlers; 11 fixtures and a manifest in `tests/fixtures/cv/` from `scripts/make_cv_fixtures.py` (standard library only, byte-for-byte reproducible). Choices: Word entries limited to 16 MiB each and 32 MiB total by declared size; encrypted PDFs refused even with an empty password; a file together with non-blank text is refused; plain text means valid UTF-8 without control characters. Code and security reviews done, no critical findings, cheap fixes applied. Left for the user: (a) `lxml` arrives with the approved `python-docx` (compiled, bundles libxml2; python-docx parses with entity resolution off and parsing runs in the killed child), recorded in ADR-089, say if you object; (b) advisories for pypdf 6.20.0 and lxml 6.1.3 could not be checked offline, so run a dependency audit (e.g. `uvx pip-audit`) when convenient; (c) the deadline test allows 2.0 s for the child to start, which could be tight on a very loaded machine (raising it is a test change, so it waits for your OK); (d) for M3.4: call `sniff_type`/`extract_text` from a worker thread through a service, pick the parse timeout knowing each spawn costs about 0.6 s, decide whether pasted text with stray control characters should be cleaned rather than refused, consider extracting Word headers and footers; a document whose text exceeds 16 MiB gets the "damaged" message; the parser child has no memory limit and can be orphaned only until its CPU limit if the app is killed hard.

Mutation check: 17 schema mutants each fail a test; FTS stayed consistent over 4,000 random operations. Code review done; fixes applied before the migration was committed. Left for the user: (a) a fact's text can't be erased: versions and events are append-only and facts can't be deleted, "discarded" is the only removal, and removing an import keeps its drafts' text; an erase feature would need a small later migration; (b) `cv_imports` stores no original file name (privacy) and only the states uploaded and done, with failure reasons read from the task; (c) `roles` has no link to the import that created them; (d) the CV quote for an imported draft is stored in `original_answer`, and `needs_check` is a flag without a reason; (e) soft-deleted capture notes and refine answers stay in the database until a later purge; (f) fact ids are text with a separate `number` for `F12@3`.
  - Checkpoint: after — first schema for stored personal data; a committed migration is never edited.

## M4 — Job intake, inbox and companies

Status: accepted (2026-10-20)

Branch: `m4-job-inbox`. Decisions for this plan: ADR-098 (eleven larger tasks with split points as in ADR-089 item 1; no new runtime dependency: stdlib `html.parser`, `ipaddress` and `json`, `httpx` already approved, no `nh3` because no AI Markdown is rendered; the page fetch runs inside the Add-job POST; Apply marks a job "To apply" and M5 creates the application; one Add job makes up to two requests through the budget gate (TD-055); the fit band is computed in code from fact ids actually sent; `summarize_job` reads no preferences; "Processing" is derived from the task state, never stored; out of M4: learning from removals, auto-hide and removal rules (M9), company contacts and history (M5), the company brief (M8) and the watch toggle (M10); single-key shortcuts get a Settings switch-off; pre-approved mechanical test updates). Security review on M4.1, M4.3, M4.4, M4.7, M4.8, M4.10, M4.11; prompt review on M4.4, M4.5; UX review on M4.7–M4.10; code review on every task. Split points if a task stalls: M4.1 (page text), M4.3 (duplicates), M4.4 (normalization and the unknown-values queue), M4.7 (triage actions and shortcuts), M4.8 (editable fields and Summarize again).

- [x] M4.1 Fetch module: SSRF guard, guarded fetcher and page text
  - AC: AC-M4.1; spec 2.9 rule 6, ADR-020, ADR-036; add-provider skill; spike first. The guard (`app/modules/fetch/guard.py`, critical coverage from M4) accepts only http and https URLs whose host resolves only to global unicast addresses; it refuses private, loopback, link-local, multicast, unspecified, shared (100.64/10), reserved and IPv4-mapped/6to4/NAT64 forms of them, numeric host spellings (decimal, hex, octal, short forms) and userinfo tricks; it checks every redirect hop again (at most 5) and connects to the address it checked, keeping the Host header and the TLS server name, so a second DNS answer can't redirect it. `GuardedJobPageFetcher` in `app/modules/fetch/providers/` is the only `httpx` user (the import contract is narrowed to it): injected resolver, no cookies, `trust_env=False` (no proxies or .netrc), a 5 MB cap counted on decoded bytes, a 15 s overall deadline including DNS, text content types only; HTTP error statuses raise `FetchRefused`, connection failures and the deadline raise `NetworkUnavailable`; the URL is never logged. `page_text(page)` uses stdlib `html.parser`, is exported from the provider-free interface, drops script, style, nav and hidden parts, keeps headings, paragraphs and lists, and prefers a JSON-LD `JobPosting` description. An app-selectable `FakeJobPageFetcher` serves fictional fixture pages for `*.example.test` only and is built by wiring only when fakes are allowed (tests, demo). Fictional fixture pages with a manifest in `tests/fixtures/jobs/`: plain HTML, JSON-LD, a JS-only shell, a Latin-1 page, a prompt-injection posting, the same ad on two "boards", a reposted ad with small edits, another role at the same company, the same title at another company. No new runtime dependency; stop for the user if pinning needs `httpcore` imported directly (ADR-098 item 2).

- [x] M4.3 Intake, snapshot and duplicates
  - AC: AC-M4.2, AC-M4.5, AC-M4.1 at the route. `JobIntakeService.add_url`: guard, fetch through the injected `JobPageFetcher`, `page_text`, then store the snapshot with the final URL and capture time; `add_text` takes pasted text with an optional link to the original (http or https only, stored, never fetched). Job text is cleaned of control, bidi and zero-width characters before storage (TD-029 and TD-168, for jobs); text over 50,000 characters is refused, never cut; a page with too little text answers "paste the job text instead". URLs are canonicalized (fragment and tracking parameters dropped); the same URL again adds a source, not a job. Near-duplicates are found by mechanical similarity of the cleaned text (reusing `app/domain/similarity.py`) with a conservative threshold set from the fixtures (a wrong merge is worse than a miss); a duplicate merges into the existing card as another source ("also posted on N sites"); "Not a duplicate" restores the job as its own card and remembers the pair. The job is stored even without a provider and stays triageable (starting its summary arrives in M4.4). Routes: `POST /inbox/intake` (token and origin; a refused or failed fetch answers with the paste-text message, opens the text box and keeps the URL as the link) and `POST /inbox/jobs/{id}/not-duplicate`. New errors (for example `JobTextNotFound`, `JobTextTooLong`, `JobNotFound`) get user-facing copy; new routes use the id shape, not `:path` (TD-210), and a plain-text 413 (TD-179).

- [x] M4.4 `summarize_job`, structured fields, normalization and companies
  - AC: AC-M4.4 (service), AC-M4.8 (data), AC-M4.9 (service); add-ai-task and background-task skills. `prompts/summarize_job/`: Fast tier; the job text goes through the untrusted filter; output is a 2–3 line summary, title, company, industry, department or team, work mode, locations, employment type, seniority, experience required, salary, posted date, and requirements each marked must-have, key or nice-to-have; no URL fields; the controlled values are an input, and values that came from job text are delimited; registered as `TaskSpec("summarize_job")`. Task `summarize_job:<job id>` starts after intake through the budget gate ("Not now" keeps the job unsummarized, as in M3.4); "Summarize again" goes through the gate too. Summary, requirements, normalized fields, company link and queue entries are written with task success in the one `ctx.complete` transaction, as a new `job_summaries` row each time; user-corrected fields are kept. Salary and experience numbers must be written in the posting (checked with `extract_quantities`), otherwise the field is left empty. Values are normalized to controlled lists by value or alias, ignoring case, spacing and accents; unknown values are stored as written and queued for mapping. Companies are matched by a name key (case, punctuation and legal suffixes ignored) or created. After a summary, the duplicate check runs again by company and title. Fake scripts are chosen by keyword; their output is labelled "Practice data (fake provider)" and says the posting wasn't read (ADR-088, ADR-089 item 8). Fixtures: ok, malformed, adversarial (an injected instruction reflected in fields, a URL in the summary, a salary the posting doesn't state, an extra `status` field) and the prompt-injection posting. The task timeout is longer than the provider call (TD-047).

- [x] M4.5 `assess_fit`, fit bands and deal-breaker flags
  - AC: AC-M4.3 (logic); add-ai-task skill; spec 3.1; ADR-098 item 6. `prompts/assess_fit/`: Fast tier; inputs are the requirements (derived from job text, so delimited), the code-owned fact block from `facts_for_ai`, and the preferences (the user's own free text, delimited as untrusted); output is each requirement met or missing, with `fact_ids` required when met, and deal-breaker and must-have conflicts by preference line number with a quote; no band and no prose about the user; registered as `TaskSpec("assess_fit")`. `app/domain/fit.py` computes the band: Strong = all must-haves and at least three quarters of key requirements met; Stretch = any must-have missing or fewer than half the key requirements met; otherwise Possible. A "met" requirement citing any id that wasn't sent counts as missing. Mechanical flag: salary below the minimum in the same currency and period (the employment-type flag was dropped by the user, 2026-10-19, ADR-098 M4.2 checkpoint update). Task `assess_fit:<job id>` runs after each successful summary and from "Assess fit again" through the gate; it is skipped with no request when no fact is usable ("Add confirmed facts to see how you match"). A new `job_assessments` row is written each time, with the fact references and versions used. Jobs with deal-breakers are flagged "Conflicts with your preferences", never hidden. The never-use request-log test is extended to both new tasks through their real calling services (ADR-098 item 9).

- [x] M4.7 Inbox screen and keyboard triage
  - AC: AC-M4.6, AC-M4.7, AC-M4.8, AC-M4.2 in the UI; htmx-ui skill. `/inbox` replaces the placeholder (placeholder tests updated mechanically, ADR-098 item 9). "+ Add job" takes a link or text, shows "Fetching the page…" while it works, and on refusal opens the text box with the paste-text message. Layout: the queue beside one focused card, one column at phone width. Card anatomy from the design: title; company, industry and department or team; work mode, salary, experience required, posting age and source; fit band in text and the 2–3 line summary; met and missing requirement chips and "Conflicts with your preferences"; the "Practice data" badge; triage buttons with their letters. The Processing state follows live task updates. A shown card is marked seen by a POST once displayed, never by a GET; the prefetched next card is not marked. Snooze sits in a keyboard-reachable card menu; Remove opens one-tap reason chips (skippable) with Undo for 10 seconds, and the Removed view is the lasting way back; A marks "To apply" with Undo. `app/static/js/shortcuts.js` handles J, K, A, R and Enter (Esc closes); shortcuts are ignored while typing, listed in a `?` help, and switched off by a versioned Settings toggle "Use single-key shortcuts". ## M5 — Applications board, tracking and Today

Status: accepted (2026-10-24)

Accepted by the user without running the manual checks, explicitly deferred to M6 acceptance. Automated verification passed; manual checks remain unperformed in `docs/acceptance/M6.md`. Final evidence and limits: `docs/acceptance/M5.md`.

Branch: `m5-applications-board`. Decisions for this plan: ADR-102 (ten larger tasks with split points as in ADR-089 item 1; new dependencies approved: `icalendar` (runtime, imported only in `app.modules.calendar.providers.ics`) and SortableJS 1.15.x vendored (MIT, board only); one migration `0005_applications.sql`, which also creates an application in Preparing for each visible to-apply job (ADR-098 item 3); Apply creates the application in the same transaction and Undo or Back to queue deletes it only while it is untouched; a status's phase lives on the status, never on the application; any status may move to any other and every move is recorded; `applied_at` is set once, on the first move into an active status (freezing is M6); events are stored in UTC with their IANA time zone, defaulting to the computer's (from `/etc/localtime`), and the form asks when it is unknown; reminders are computed when read and nothing is scheduled (ADR-013); notifications hold due reminders plus a link to tasks needing attention; the .ics download is a token-protected GET fetched and saved by a script; out of M5: prep state (M8), suggested session (M7), document drafts (M6), Tailoring and Prep tabs (M6, M8), an AI-drafted thank-you note, rejection feedback into the fact bank, deleting contacts, notes or interactions (events are cancelled instead), company merge (TD-257); default reminder rule "Applied for 14 days" on; re-apply note over a fixed 30 days; pre-approved mechanical test updates). Security review on M5.1, M5.2, M5.4, M5.5, M5.6, M5.8, M5.9, M5.10; UX review on M5.4–M5.9; code review on every task; no prompt review (no AI task). Tasks run strictly in sequence (shared `main.py`, `wiring.py`, `app.css`, shell templates). Split points if a task stalls: M5.2 (domain after the migration), M5.4 (table view; drag after the status menu), M5.5 (events and the download after the drawer and timeline), M5.6 (company history after contacts), M5.7 (Settings > Board after the reminders service). Pre-approved mechanical test updates (ADR-102 item 11): Board (M5.4) and Today (M5.9) join the own-page list in `test_placeholder_pages_have_empty_state` and `test_empty_state_explanations_differ_per_area`; `test_every_page_path_dispatches_to_its_page_without_token` gets a seeded application id; the leak-scan page map in `tests/leak_scan.py` gets the new pages; `test_company_page_has_no_placeholder_for_unbuilt_features` drops its "contact", "history" and "application" absences once those sections exist (the "brief", "watch", "coming soon", "later version" and "will appear" absences stay); Tab counts from the page start in `tests/e2e/test_shell.py` and similar tests gain one Tab for the Notifications button (M5.8); the To-apply card test in `tests/integration/test_inbox.py` is extended for the "Open application" link with its existing assertions kept.

- [x] M5.1 Spike: .ics calendar provider with time zones, vendored SortableJS, the computer's zone name
  - AC: AC-M5.4 (generation); add-provider skill; ADR-019, ADR-031, ADR-102; risky unknowns first. `icalendar` joins the runtime dependencies and is imported only in `app/modules/calendar/providers/ics.py` under a new forbidden contract (no existing contract loosened); the run stops for the user if the lock adds more than `python-dateutil`, `six` and `tzdata`, or if the installed version cannot build a VTIMEZONE from `zoneinfo`. `IcsCalendarProvider(clock)`: `name="ics"`, `supports_sync=False`, `sync_event` raises `CalendarSyncUnsupported`; `export_event` writes one VCALENDAR (VERSION 2.0, PRODID, METHOD PUBLISH) with one VEVENT: UID `<event id>@jobpipeline.invalid`, DTSTAMP from the injected clock, SEQUENCE from the event version, DTSTART/DTEND with TZID and a generated VTIMEZONE or `VALUE=DATE` for all-day deadlines, SUMMARY/DESCRIPTION/LOCATION escaped and folded by the library, STATUS:CANCELLED for cancelled events. The filename is built in code from the event type and local date (`interview-2026-11-03.ics`), never from user text. `EventData` gains `sequence`, `all_day`, `location` and `cancelled`. `system_time_zone_name()` in `app/wiring.py` reads the IANA name from the `/etc/localtime` link and checks it with `zoneinfo`, returning None when unknown. - [x] M5.2 Applications schema and domain
  - AC: groundwork for AC-M5.1 to AC-M5.6; db-migration skill. One migration `0005_applications.sql` holds all M5 schema (later fixes go forward as 0006): `application_statuses` (key, label, `phase` NOT NULL CHECK `preparing|active|closed`, position, built_in; seeded Preparing; Applied, Screening, Interviewing, Offer; Rejected, Withdrawn, Accepted, Declined); `applications` (`job_id` unique FK, status FK, `applied_at`; phase read through the status, never stored); append-only `status_history` (application, nullable from-status, to-status, changed_at, source `created|board|menu|drawer|backfill`; update and delete refused by triggers); `application_notes` (kind `note|feedback`); `contacts` (company FK, name, role, email, phone, relationship, notes); `application_contacts` (unique pair); `interactions` (contact, optional application, happened_at, channel `email|call|video|in_person|message|other`, note); `events` (type `interview|follow_up|deadline|offer_deadline`, starts_at/ends_at UTC, all_day, `time_zone` NOT NULL, round, format, interviewers, location, thank_you_reminder, status `scheduled|cancelled`); `reminder_rules` (status FK, days 1–365, enabled; seeded "Applied for 14 days", on); `reminder_states` (unique occurrence key, `open|done|dismissed|snoozed`, snooze_until, seen_at). Every mutable table has `created_at`, `updated_at`, `version`; indexes for board columns, table sorts, due reminders and Today. Backfill: each visible (not merged) `to_apply` job gets one Preparing application and a `backfill` history row, ids made in SQL in the safe-id shape. No external-calendar-id table yet (`ExternalRef` exists). Pure domain `app/domain/applications.py`: a custom status needs a phase and a non-empty unique label; built-ins can be renamed, not deleted; any status may move to any other and every move records history; `applied_at` is set the first time an application enters an active status and never cleared; entering `interviewing` offers an optional date, entering `rejected` optional feedback. `app/domain/events.py`: local wall time plus zone to UTC; a spring-forward gap time is refused with a message; a repeated autumn hour takes fold 0 and says so; all-day dates; next event and last activity; the zone object is passed in (no I/O).

- [x] M5.7 Reminder rules, due reminders and Settings > Board
  - AC: AC-M5.5 (logic), AC-M5.1 (Settings UI); ADR-013. `app/domain/reminders.py` (pure, frozen clock and zone) builds occurrences with stable keys: rules (status entered N or more days ago, by its history row; one occurrence per status entry); events (follow-ups, deadlines and offer deadlines due at their time, all-day at local midnight; a thank-you reminder due at the interview's end). A status change ends the old status's rule occurrences; cancelled events make none. Done and Dismiss end an occurrence; Snooze (Tomorrow or Next week) hides it until a local date. `ReminderService` computes due reminders on read; nothing is scheduled (no task type, no timer) and a closed app changes nothing. Settings > Board: statuses added (with a phase), renamed, moved up or down, deleted (unused custom only); reminder rules per status ("Remind me if an application stays in <status> for N days", on or off); both versioned with the 409 reload prompt and field-tied 422s; routes need token and origin.

## M6 — Tailoring, honesty enforcement and documents

Status: accepted (2026-10-28)

Accepted on the user's instruction without running the manual checks, explicitly deferred until after M7 acceptance. Automated verification passed at ec6c4cb. The manual checks, the carried M5 checks and the opt-in live test `test_m6_schemas_accepted_by_real_service` remain unperformed in `docs/acceptance/M6.md`. Open limits are listed there and in `docs/TECH_DEBT.md`.

Acceptance carry-over: preserve and complete the unchecked M5 manual checks in `docs/acceptance/M6.md` alongside M6's own acceptance criteria. User-approved deferral: 2026-10-24.

Branch: `m6-tailoring-documents` (stacked on `m5-applications-board`; local only). Decisions for this plan: ADR-106 (twelve larger tasks with split points as in ADR-089 item 1, ADR-098 item 1 and ADR-102 item 1; the guideline is unchanged; the renderer spike M6.1 compares WeasyPrint, Playwright/Chromium print, ReportLab, fpdf2 and typst on macOS and Linux (ADR-061) in scratch environments and records ADR-107, and no PDF dependency enters `pyproject.toml` before the user picks at the M6.1 checkpoint; Word export uses the approved `python-docx`, with exactly `app.render.providers.word -> docx` added to the docx contract, services forbidding `app.render.providers` and the API forbidding `app.render`; Linux is checked by a local Docker run of the renderer suite (M6.2, acceptance) and CI gets the system libraries but runs only after a push; one migration `0006_tailoring.sql` holds all M6 schema and recreates the Undo guard so any tailoring row makes an application "touched"; contact details are a versioned setting `personal_details` (name, email, phone, location, up to three http/https links, photo reference), filled into headers and sign-offs by code and never sent to the AI, with no date of birth, nationality or marital status; the photo is JPEG or PNG by content, at most 5 MB and 100–4000 px a side, metadata stripped by a pure segment filter, stored 0600 under `assets/` with a generated name, no image library; the master CV is built by code from `facts_for_ai` and the diff aligns lines by fact ids; the country defaults to `JobPreferences.cv_country` with a per-application select, and each CV version snapshots its template; TD-043: a code-owned honesty notice (wording in ADR-106) is added by the executor for generation and feedback tasks; generated units: a cited id not sent in that request or a content unit without ids is `unsupported`, quantities, names, skills and model-reported details absent from the unit's cited facts or a fact of another role are `needs_check`, a model structure unit with text beyond a template heading is uncited content, and approval also flags citations to facts now never-use, archived, discarded or superseded; confirming a flagged unit or "Save this as a fact?" creates a confirmed fact the unit then cites (spec 3.1 item 4; the design's "claims typed into CVs" drafts wording is updated); gap-check verdicts and the "may not be worth applying for" sentence come from code with the fit rules; quick questions become an inline "Add a fact" (AI questioning in M7); each step is one user-started request through the budget gate, never chained (TD-055 unchanged; TD-171 applies); untrusted inputs are single JSON blocks (TD-045 open); requirements stay as summarized (TD-258 open); export is a POST writing 0600 files with code-built names under `documents/<application id>/`, download a token-protected read-only GET through the fetch-and-save helper (extends the M5.5 .ics exception); the first active move freezes, in the same transaction as `applied_at`, the latest exported approved version of each document (else the latest approved, else none); "used in" links, sent documents in the drawer and document drafts on Today are in M6; the Tailoring tab is `/applications/{id}?tab=tailoring` (Timeline and contacts stays the default, no Interview prep placeholder), its resume step derived and GETs writing nothing; documents are written in the facts' language; the critical-coverage registry is unchanged). Code review on every task; security review on M6.1, M6.2, M6.4–M6.12; prompt review on M6.3, M6.5, M6.6, M6.7, M6.9; UX review on M6.5, M6.6, M6.8–M6.12. Tasks run in sequence (shared `main.py`, `wiring.py`, `app.css`, application templates, `TASK_SPECS`, fake scripts); only M6.3 may run in a separate worktree alongside M6.1–M6.2 (disjoint files, one coordinator). Split points if a task stalls: M6.1 (PDF report after the Word renderer and contract suite), M6.2 (photo and Docker/CI after text and layout), M6.3 (notice after the checker), M6.4 (domain after the migration), M6.5 (angles after the gap check), M6.6 (templates after details and photo), M6.7 (regenerate, edit and confirm after generate and check), M6.8 (edit, save-as-fact and regenerate UI after diff and approve), M6.9 (screen after service), M6.10 (Submit and freeze after export and downloads), M6.11 (drawer, then Today after "used in"). Pre-approved mechanical test updates (ADR-102 item 11 style): `test_ac_m3_4_every_ai_task_has_a_never_use_request_log_case` gains the five M6 tasks through their calling services (draft_cv_template's case asserts no fact text at all); tests asserting the exact request system text of `refine_fact` and `assess_fit` gain the honesty-notice prefix only (golden render files unchanged); the "Open workspace" href assertion in `tests/integration/test_application_detail.py` becomes `/applications/{id}?tab=tailoring` with its other assertions kept; the stored-tables allowlists in the M3–M5 cross-cutting leak tests gain the M6 tables and the `personal_details` setting; Tab counts from the page start in application-page e2e tests gain the tab-list stops; `test_every_page_path_dispatches_to_its_page_without_token` gets seeded ids if a new page route appears. Decisions made by implementers, listed here and not yet in an ADR: (a) `merge_regenerated` "in place" means relative order, so a kept unit moves earlier when the new run is shorter; (b) facts match by fact number, ignoring version; (c) a new unit is dropped only when its fact-number set equals a kept or removed unit's; (d) `cv_diff` shows a multi-fact line as ADDED and marks its sources REMOVED; (e) role order is most recent first; (f) limits: name and location 100 characters, email 254, phone 30 with 6 to 15 digits, up to 3 links of 300 characters, headings 1 to 40; (g) an approved `cv_template` may return to draft by UPDATE, so an edit resets it for re-approval; (h) draft to sent is refused. (4) The M6.6 form route must not log or render pydantic error text or input values; map errors to field names only, and test that a refused form never echoes the entered name, email or phone. (5) Exported files embed contact details from the current setting, so M6.10's freeze must keep the exported file and not re-render it. (6) Renderers treat links as data, and nothing may fetch them.

Fragments `/applications/{id}/tailoring/{step}` need the token; POSTs need token, origin and version. `gap_check` (Thorough, feedback; dedupe `gap_check:<application id>`): job context with requirement ids from the current summary and the `facts_for_ai` block. It returns met, partial or missing per requirement with fact ids, plus framing ideas citing fact ids. Verdicts use the fit rules (a citation counts only if sent); a missing must-have makes code say "Your confirmed facts don't show N of this role's must-haves: … It may not be worth applying for."; framing claims absent from cited facts show "This isn't in your fact bank."; each gap offers an inline "Add a fact" (confirmed at once) and "Check again". `propose_angles` (Thorough, feedback; dedupe per application): job context, facts and up to ten angles chosen in other applications (one JSON untrusted block); returns 2–3 angles with a one-line rationale, cited ids and an optional reused angle id (unknown ids ignored). The user picks, edits or writes one, saved as an angle on the workspace. Without a summary or confirmed facts the step says what to do first. Starts go through the budget gate; results are written with task success; fake output labelled.

## M7 — Enrichment sessions and coverage

Status: planned

Acceptance carry-over: the M5 and M6 manual checks and the M6 opt-in live test `test_m6_schemas_accepted_by_real_service` remain unperformed and will be copied unchecked from `docs/acceptance/M6.md` into `docs/acceptance/M7.md` alongside M7's own checks. On 2026-10-29 the user approved continuing M7 and deferred manual checks until the end of M7 or possibly M8; this is not evidence that any check passed.

Branch: `m7-enrichment-sessions` (stacked on `m6-tailoring-documents`; local only). Decisions for this plan: ADR-117 (ten larger tasks with split points as in ADR-106 item 1; requirement clusters computed on read in code, by controlled skill then content-word similarity, with no AI task; strength from current fit and gap-check citations plus skill tags, one story counts as Thin; session drafts checked only against the user's own answers, the target note or fact and the chosen role; leading questions replaced by a code-owned open question; two user-started Fast requests per question; cooldowns 14/21/30 days; one open session; rejection feedback never sent to the AI; pre-approved mechanical test updates). Code review on every task; security review on M7.3–M7.10; prompt review on M7.4, M7.5 and M7.10; UX review on M7.6–M7.9. Tasks run in sequence (shared `main.py`, `wiring.py`, `TASK_SPECS`, fake scripts, `app.css`, fact-bank and application templates); only M7.2 may run in its own worktree alongside M7.1 (disjoint pure-domain files, one coordinator). Split points if a task stalls: M7.1 (evidence and strength after clustering), M7.2 (suggestion timing and quality hints after scoring and outcomes), M7.3 (repositories after the migration), M7.4 (outcomes after question generation), M7.5 (follow-ups and capture-note resolution after the first draft), M7.6 (end summary after the question/answer panel), M7.7 (Strengthen after the table and Start session), M7.8 (Today after Settings), M7.9 (capture notes and quality hints after gap check, interview and rejection), M7.10 (docs and acceptance after the canaries and performance).

- [x] M7.1 Spike: requirement clusters and coverage strength
  - AC: AC-M7.5 (strength definitions, domain); groundwork for AC-M7.1 (demand signal) and AC-M7.5 (job counts); risky unknown first. Pure `app/domain/coverage.py`: `cluster_requirements(requirements, skills)` groups the requirements of saved and active jobs. A requirement naming a controlled skill or alias as a whole phrase (`normalize_skill` folding) joins that skill's cluster. Otherwise requirements are grouped by Dice similarity of their content words (stopwords and punctuation removed; threshold fixed in this task from fictional fixtures, starting at 0.6), assigned deterministically, with order-independent output. Cluster label: the most frequent wording (ties: shortest, then alphabetical). Cluster key: `skill:<normalized>` or `text:<signature>`, and cluster id = the first 12 hex digits of its SHA-256 (safe-id shape, used in URLs). `strength(evidence)`: Strong = 2 or more confirmed stories; Thin = at least one confirmed fact or story but fewer than two stories; None = none. Draft, archived, discarded and never-use facts don't count. Read-only `coverage_repo`: requirements of each job's current summary for "saved" jobs (visible root jobs that are New, Seen, Snoozed or To apply) and for jobs whose application is preparing or active (closed applications, removed and merged jobs excluded), with kind and job state. Evidence comes from the current assessment's met citations, the latest gap check's met or partial citations, and fact skills. One query per source; no N+1. Measure clustering of 1,000 fictional jobs × 10 requirements. If the median exceeds 100 ms, add a cache keyed by a data stamp (latest summary, assessment, gap-check and fact-skill change) in a service object built in `app/main.py`, with no module-level state. Record the threshold, the measured times and the limits in ADR-118.

- [x] M7.2 Backlog priority, outcomes, scheduling, quality hints and session settings (domain)
  - AC: AC-M7.1, AC-M7.4, AC-M7.6 (domain); design "Backlog priority", "Quality hints", "Frequency controls". Pure `app/domain/enrichment.py`: backlog items have the kinds `cluster`, `quality_hint` (fact) and `capture_note`, each with a stable target key. `score(item, signals, state, now)`: demand = for each job asking, job weight (saved job 1, application preparing 2, active 3, interview event in the next 14 days 4) × requirement weight (must-have 3, key 2, nice 1). Weakness raises the score: None above Thin, Thin above quality hints; Strong clusters enter only for a focused session. A capture note adds a fixed raise. Items in cooldown are left out (they come back only when nothing else is left). "Next session" items go first. Expired items are never offered. `order_backlog` is total and deterministic (ties by key). `record_outcome(state, outcome, now, zone, later_choice)`: answered → 30-day cooldown; skipped → 14 days; not sure ("I don't remember") → 21 days, counted, earlier wording kept (at most 5) for a different angle; later → next session, +7 local days, +1 calendar month (clamped) or a date after today. `expired(item, signals)`: cluster with no saved or active job; note deleted or resolved; fact no longer confirmed, now never-use, or the hint no longer applies. `app/domain/quality_hints.py`: "Add a result?" (story without a result), "Add a number?" (no quantity per `extract_quantities`), "Add what you did?" (story without an action); never for never-use or unconfirmed facts. `SessionSettings` in `app/domain/settings.py` (`SESSIONS` spec, versioned): frequency `never|weekly|twice_weekly|after_application` (default weekly), length 3|5 (default 3), `ask_later_default` `next_session|week|month` (default week), `paused` (default false). `suggestion_due(settings, last_reset, last_applied_at, now)`: weekly = 7 days; twice a week = 84 hours; after each application = an `applied_at` after the last reset; never or paused = never.

One migration `0007_sessions.sql`. `enrichment_questions` (backlog state only: id; kind `cluster|quality_hint|capture_note`; target `cluster_key` | `fact_id` FK | `capture_note_id` FK, exactly one, CHECK; unique per kind and target; `status` `open|expired`; `cooldown_until`; `later_until`; `later_next_session` 0/1; `not_sure_count`; `asked_count`; `last_outcome`; `last_asked_at`; `earlier_wordings` JSON array ≤5; created_at, updated_at, version). `sessions` (id; `focus_kind` `most_useful|cluster|job|role|skill|fact|capture_note`; `focus_ref` text; optional `job_id` FK; `length` 3|5; `state` `open|ended`; `started_at`, `ended_at`; version; a partial unique index allows one open session). `session_answers` (id; session_id; position; question_id FK; `follow_up_of`; question text and why, with the question's provider, model and prompt provenance and a `fallback` flag; `answer_text` ≤ 4,000 characters; `outcome` `pending|answered|skipped|not_sure|later`; `later_choice`; `draft_fact_id` FK; `missing` and `flagged_claims` JSON; structure provenance; question and structure task ids (ON DELETE SET NULL as in 0003); created_at, updated_at, version). - [x] M7.4 Session service, `enrichment_question` and outcomes
  - AC: AC-M7.2, AC-M7.4, AC-M7.1 (service); spec 3.3, 3.6; add-ai-task and background-task skills. `SessionService.start(focus, length)` refuses while another session is open (it offers that session) and when nothing is left to ask ("Nothing to ask right now…"). It goes through the budget gate ("A 3-question session uses up to 6 requests"). The backlog comes from `coverage_repo` + `enrichment_repo` + `order_backlog`, filtered by focus: a cluster; a job's clusters, missing ones first; a role's facts and hints; a skill's cluster; a fact; a capture note. `enrichment_question` (Fast, feedback; dedupe `session:<id>`; `input_ref` holds ids and tier only) gets, each as one JSON untrusted block: the item (cluster label and up to 5 requirement wordings, the hint and its fact, or the note text), related facts through `facts_for_ai`/`prompt_fact_block` (generalize kept), the session so far, and earlier wordings ("ask from a different angle"). It returns `question` (≤300 characters) and `why` (≤200). Pure `app/domain/questions.py`: `question_problems(question, sources, known_skills)` flags quantities, names and skills missing from the sources (`new_claims`), yes/no openers (Did, Were, Was, Have, Has, Is, Are, Do, Does, Can, Could, Would), tag endings ("right?", "correct?", "didn't you?", "isn't it?") and missing question form. A question with problems is replaced by a code-owned open question for the item ("Tell me about a time you worked on <label>.", with its why), marked `fallback`, with no extra request. Results are written with task success. Outcomes without AI (`skip`, `not_sure`, `later` with its menu, `end`) are version-checked and update item state through `record_outcome`. Fake output is labelled.

- [x] M7.5 `structure_answer`, drafts with the original answer and follow-ups
  - AC: AC-M7.3; spec 3.1 item 5, 3.2, 3.3; ADR-089 items 5 and 8, ADR-097; add-ai-task and background-task skills. Answer (≤4,000 characters, version-checked, through the budget gate) → `structure_answer` (Fast, feedback; dedupe `session:<id>`). Untrusted blocks: the question, the user's answers to this item in this session, the capture note or the hint's target fact, and the existing role labels and controlled skills. It returns `type` `fact|story`, `text` or situation/action/result, an optional role key from the roles given, skills, `missing` (closed list: result, number, scale, own_part, context) and `added_details`. Pure `resolve_structured_answer`: the honesty check (`new_claims`) uses only these allowed sources: this item's answers, the note text, the target fact and the chosen role label. Flagged claims and `added_details` set `needs_check` and are shown as "This isn't in your answer". Skills not named in the answer are dropped; an unknown role key is cleared and flagged. The draft is written with `create_drafts_in` (source `session`, provenance, `original_answer` = the answers verbatim) and the answer row (answer text, draft link, missing, flags) in `ctx.complete`. When parts are missing, at most one follow-up per item is generated through `enrichment_question` with the missing parts; it counts toward the length. Its answer adds a new version of the same draft (`original_answer` holds both answers) and never confirms it. A capture-note item sets the note's `resolved_fact_id` to the draft. Never-use target facts are never sent or used. Fake output is labelled "Practice data (fake provider)".
  - Tests: integration golden `test_structure_answer_prompt_golden`, `test_ac_m7_3_answer_produces_draft_and_keeps_original_answer[fact|story]`, `test_ac_m7_3_original_answer_kept_on_answer_row_and_version`, `test_structure_answer_invented_claim_flags_draft[number|employer|skill|added_details]`, `test_question_text_is_not_an_allowed_source`, `test_structure_answer_unknown_role_cleared_and_unnamed_skill_dropped`, `test_structure_answer_injection_in_answer_changes_nothing`, `test_structure_answer_schema_refuses_status_field`, `test_structure_answer_malformed_retry_then_invalid`, `test_follow_up_asks_for_missing_part_and_revises_same_draft`, `test_draft_written_with_task_success_or_not_at_all`, `test_capture_note_item_resolves_note_on_draft`, `test_fake_session_drafts_labelled_practice_data`; pre-approved never-use case.

- [x] M7.6 Session screen and end-of-session summary
  - AC: AC-M7.3, AC-M7.4 (UI); design "Enrichment sessions"; htmx-ui skill. `GET /sessions/new` (token-free page; focus and length form with defaults from Settings) and `POST /sessions` (token, origin; budget dialog pattern). `GET /sessions/{id}` is a token-free page by safe id (spec 2.9 rules 12–13, as fact pages). The panel shows "Question n of N", the question, its "Why this question" line, the answer box and the buttons Submit answer, Skip, "I don't remember", "Ask me later" (menu: next session, in a week, in a month, pick a date; default from Settings) and End session. While structuring, the panel waits and is polled (refine-panel pattern); task events refresh it, and typing is kept in page memory (M4.8 pattern). The live draft below shows the draft's wording, missing parts in words, flagged claims and the practice label. The fact bank gets a "Start session" button. End summary: each draft with Confirm, Edit (editor link; flagged drafts confirm only individually, ADR-091), Discard, "Merge into F12" for hint items, and "Leave in Drafts to review". Focus moves deliberately and outcomes are announced.
